2. **Quantity reset (COND-M08, owner note k).** Decided by the owner: the quantity reset is NOT ported; recorded as a possible Windows defect: silent data loss (Windows replaces a stored quantity that the option list of the type does not offer by "(none)" on every save of that condition; for an unknown type the option list is always the Count list, so it hits unknown types too). **[Updated 2026-11-27 (parity pass 2): Windows 0a24294 now keeps a stored quantity the style does not offer (it skips unchanged quantity rows), so this is parity, not a defect; the owner decision stands as parity and the pin tests were added.]**
3. **Two duplicates at 2147483646 (DLG-FINITE-01).** Decided by the owner: two duplicates at 2147483646 are pinned in Core (`refuse_new_condition_numbers(2147483646, 2)` gives X = 2147483648, plural) and by sequential store calls, and are not reachable through the UI (Duplicate copies one condition per request).
4. **Stray quote in the elevation text (COND-ELEV-01, possible Windows defect (1)).** Decided by the owner: a single stray ASCII `"` anywhere in the elevation text switches the closing-inch-mark rule off: recorded as a possible Windows defect and ported as is (confirmed by the owner). **WITHDRAWN 2026-11-27 (job 9):** Windows `0a24294` fixed it (a stray quote no longer hides text after a foot mark: `5'x"`, `5'6x"`, `5'abc"`, `5'x1/2"`, `10'6.3"` and `6"5'7` are refused; `edit_condition_dialog.py:184-186` and `:1622-1651` at `d26893c`), so the reason for porting the quirk is gone and iOS ported the Windows rule in job 9 (`Core/Domain/ConditionFormRules.cpp:70-107`; commits `2e804e1`, `b4662a8`). The decision "ported as is" is void; the owner may object (it overturns a decision of 2026-11-21 only because its premise, the Windows behaviour, changed).
5. **Stale Windows line-number citations in four comment files.** Decided by the owner: the stale Windows line-number citations in four iOS comment files are left as they are (`Core/Domain/CameraFraming.hpp` :6 cites `mesh_view.py`; `Config/Tests/Settings/test_scene_rendering.cpp` :16 cites `mesh_view.py`; `Config/Tests/Persistence/test_load_fields.cpp` :86 and :88 cite `plan_view/view.py` and `test_view.py`; `Config/Tests/Settings/test_takeoff_selection.cpp` :14 cites `test_view.py`); the old -> new map of these Windows files in `PARITY_MAP.md` (section "Re-baseline 2026-11-21") stays the reference; no code comment, test or behaviour was touched.

## Closed by the owner decisions of job 11 (2026-11-28)

The owner decided three of the open questions of "Decisions for the owner after parity pass 2" on 2026-11-28 (job 11, branch `ios-parity-sync-2`; Windows `d1c430a` read only and unchanged, iOS start `fc3c3b6`). Each decision is ported test first; the iOS code commits are `8939870` (layers), `6d27c03` (Count as Negative on a group), `037c2a2` (FailureText Core module), `4223178` (import), `cdcc380` (picker, load, folder delete), `fc4b725` (export), `5884ab7` (page delete); review-fix commits `53d2a54`, `9856e4c` and `7a8b600` (the code tip) and docs commits follow (docs commits carry `[skip ci]`). The decisions overturn or replace three earlier defaults ("keep iOS" for EXP-21, "the iOS titles stay" for the layer refresh titles, "pressed takeoff only" for Count as Negative on a group); every statement of this file that said so carries an "Updated 2026-11-28 (job 11)" note and the old text stays as history.

**Decision 1 (owner question 1, RESOLVED 2026-11-28).** EXP-21 (owner question 1), import, load, export, folder-delete and page-delete alert wording: ADOPT the current Windows wording (Windows `d1c430a`: `ost_visualizer/shared/texts.py`, `ost_visualizer/presentation/texts.py`, `import_handler.py`, `export_service.py`, `presentation/utils/messagebox.py`). The decision of 2026-11-19 ("keep iOS") is SUPERSEDED (kept as history). Rule (`presentation/utils/messagebox.py:130-170`): an alert shows ONE user sentence; the technical detail (parser reason, Core diagnostic, store text, exception) goes to the log only (`NSLog` on iOS; the export paths log the reason too since the review fix 9856e4c: `ExportService.mm` writes an NSLog line for nothing to export, for a file name that is not valid UTF-8 and for an OBJ, DXF or HTML write failure, with the path and errno). It is applied to every existing iOS alert of import (picker and open-in), project-list and bid load failures, folder delete failures, the page-delete reload failure and the three export formats (table of 16 texts (rows 1-15 plus the unexpected-case row 5a) in `PARITY_MAP.md`, section "Owner decisions of job 11 (2026-11-28)"). The wording of the M3 exports (OST / OSP / CSV / PDF) is not on iOS yet: the same rule is recorded for M3 in `NEW_FEATURES_PLAN.md` section 4.15. Judgement call (confirmed by the owner's decision text): the LOCAL import path is the Windows local path (`import_handler.py:218-246`): EVERY local OST / OSP failure shows "Import Error" / "The OST file is damaged or uses an unsupported format, so it cannot be imported." (OSP: "The OSP file ..."); the per-case sentences (no bid, takeoffs invalid, invalid references) are Windows HEAD texts that reach a dialog only on the SQL import (`import_handler.py:271-278`) and are not used in the iOS alert; a store, schema or temporary-folder failure shows the same damaged-file sentence (Windows `import_ost` swallows every exception and returns False, `ost_importer.py:94-107`); the sentence "An unexpected error stopped the import of the OST|OSP file. Please check that the file is valid and try again; if the problem continues, contact support." appears only for a genuinely unexpected condition: a C++ exception caught in `ImportService.mm` (`importOSTFile:` and `importOSPFile:` wrap their bodies in try/catch) or a nil `NSError` after a failed import. Known-cause sentences (not found, access denied, disk full, "open in another program", network location) cannot be wired today (the writers return bool, the store returns text only, no errno), so only the vocabulary exists, with host tests, for M3 (`Core/Domain/FailureText.{hpp,cpp}`: `FileFailureCause`, `file_failure_reason`, `cause_from_errno`, `write_failure_text`). "Please ensure you have write permissions to the destination folder" has no destination folder on iOS (temporary folder and share sheet); it is adopted as it is (the owner said adopt the Windows wording). Core: `Core/Domain/FailureText.{hpp,cpp}` (classifier of the raw import diagnostics and the sentences of picker, open-in, load, folder delete, page delete and export) and `Core/Import/ImportDiagnostics.hpp` (the shared raw-diagnostic constants of the parsers: every diagnostic text and its tests are unchanged); `ImportService` returns an NSError in the domain `OSTImportErrorDomain` (code = the failure kind, description = the picker sentence) and logs the technical reason. Tests: Exp21_ImportDiagnostics, Exp21_ImportClassify, Exp21_ImportText, Exp21_LoadReason, Exp21_FolderDelete, Exp21_FileFailure, Exp21_ExportText (24 tests in `Config/Tests/Browser/test_failure_text.cpp`); the page-delete pin of `Config/Tests/Persistence/test_page_menu_rules.cpp` changed (`PageDialogText.DeleteReloadFailed_SaysTheDeleteHappened_AndNeverShowsTheReason`). The old -> new table of the 16 texts (rows 1-15 plus the unexpected-case row 5a) is in `PARITY_MAP.md`, section "Owner decisions of job 11 (2026-11-28)". Not changed and verified byte for byte against Windows: the browser failures (rename, delete, restore, move, create), the other layer alerts, page rename and delete, Set Scale, conditions, condition folders and "No Data"; iOS-only alerts that stay: "Not Saved", "Bid Locked", "Failed to delete the condition." and "Failed to save the display settings.".

**Decision 2 (owner question 4, RESOLVED 2026-11-28).** Layer refresh and failure alerts (owner question 4, layer part): take the Windows DIALOG titles and texts (`layers_dialog.py:489`, `:784`, `:677-694`, `:706-722`): the rename refresh-failed alert is titled "Rename Layer" and the move one "Move Layer" (was "Refresh Error" for both; the texts are unchanged: "The layer was renamed|moved, but the layer list could not be refreshed. Reopen the database to see the change."); the generic failures "Failed to create|rename|move|delete layer." LOSE the database-locked hint (the Windows dialog never has it, only the sidebar adds it), which removes the hint part of the iOS addition N-M1-17 (the alert itself, no silent failure, stays). Unchanged: the create refresh-failed alert ("Refresh Error": the Windows dialog flow itself uses it), the delete refresh-failed alert ("Delete Layer" / "Layers were deleted, but the refresh failed."), "Duplicate Layer", "Failed to validate layer usage.", the three "Layer Visibility" alerts (same title in the Windows dialog), and the iOS-only refusals (50 characters, control characters, sequence limit, "This bid is locked."). Core only (`Core/Domain/LayerSheet.cpp`), no Objective-C edit; tests `LayerSheet.RefreshFailureTextsSayWhatWasCommitted` and `LayerSheet.WriteFailureTextsAreTheWindowsDialogTexts` (edited in place, written red first). The folder-failure titles of the same question are not part of the owner's decision and are unchanged.

**Decision 3 (owner question 5, RESOLVED 2026-11-28).** "Count as Negative Quantity" on a pasted GROUP (owner question 5): the Windows rule replaces the pressed-takeoff-only default for a group; a single takeoff is unchanged. The Windows "group" is the CURRENT plan-view multi-selection (after Paste the pasted takeoffs, children included, plus annotations stay selected; annotation and unknown uids are dropped; no descendant expansion). Rule (`plan_view_action_handler.py:2134-2181` on_set_negative; `input_handler.py:3247-3251`; `view_context_menu.py:162-196`; `takeoff.py:136`): the item is offered only if the selection holds at least one root takeoff and NO child takeoff (hole or attachment); it is checked only when EVERY selected takeoff is negative; the new value is "not all negative" for EVERY selected takeoff (mixed or all positive -> all True, all negative -> all False), NOT taken from the pressed item; ONE undo entry records (uid, old) and (uid, new) for ALL members, also those already at the target; undo restores each takeoff's own old flag. Ported in Core: `Core/Domain/NegativeChange.hpp` `negative_toggle_for_press(takeoffs, group_uids, pressed_uid)` returning {offered, checked, changes}; the GROUP path (two or more uids that resolve to takeoffs and the pressed takeoff is a member) is the Windows rule, the SINGLE path (empty group, group of one, pressed takeoff not a member) is exactly the old behaviour (no hole gate: iOS still offers the item on a single pressed hole or attachment, Windows hides it: recorded divergence kept). iOS has no multi-selection and no paste before M2, so the Objective-C long-press handler passes an EMPTY group (identical behaviour through the new Core function); the M2 paste wiring passes the pasted uids as the group (`NEW_FEATURES_PLAN.md` N-M2-04, question resolved). UND-09 single-takeoff row stays (single takeoff only; the group is the Windows rule). Tests: suite `Und09_NegativeGroup` (9 tests) in `Config/Tests/Settings/test_negative_press.cpp`; the six `Und09_NegativePress` tests stay valid. Owner flags (defaults = exact Windows): a pasted group that contains a hole or attachment has no item; the check mark shows only when ALL are negative and a press sets all negative. Tests: `Und09_NegativeGroup.AnAllPositiveGroupBecomesAllNegative`, `Und09_NegativeGroup.AnAllPositiveGroupOfThreeIsOneEntryOfThree`, `Und09_NegativeGroup.AMixedGroupSetsEveryMemberNegativeAndTheNegativeOnesStayInTheEntry`, `Und09_NegativeGroup.AnAllNegativeGroupBecomesAllPositive`, `Und09_NegativeGroup.AHoleOrAnAttachmentInTheGroupHidesTheItem`, `Und09_NegativeGroup.APressedTakeoffOutsideTheGroupActsAlone`, `Und09_NegativeGroup.AnnotationAndUnknownUidsOfTheGroupAreSkipped`, `Und09_NegativeGroup.TheSinglePathIsTheOldBehaviour`, `Und09_NegativeGroup.OneUndoRestoresEachFlagOfTheGroupAndOneRedoAppliesThemAgain`.

| 5 M2, "Count as Negative Quantity" on a group | the Windows rule for a group, a single takeoff unchanged (decision 3) | RESOLVED, ported in Core (the wiring is M2) |

## Closed by the Windows commits `0a24294`..`d26893c` and the decisions of job 9 (2026-11-27)

Job 9, 2026-11-27 (parity pass 2), branch `ios-parity-sync-2`. Windows (read only, clean tree) moved from `49a2d59` to `d26893c`: 11 commits (`d26893c`, `c46d751`, `2fa4f61`, `0a24294`, `7f24e0f`, `5f94de1`, `c5065ac`, `d2caf63`, `441b45e`, `7082ddf`, `df78f67`), 413 files, 313 test functions added and 17 removed in `tests/` (net +296; `def test_` lines of the diff). All behaviour fixes of defects that iOS had reported are inside `0a24294`: its message only says "Move texts ..." but it holds about 60 logic changes and 88 new tests labelled "iOS-port defect round (conditions|folders|layers|pages)" in `tests/quality_review.json`; `CHANGELOG.md` "Unreleased / Fixed" lists them in user language. `df78f67` is paste, guides, context-menu Delete and sidebar scroll (M2 inputs, section 5.7 of `NEW_FEATURES_PLAN.md`), `7082ddf` is SQL close and recovery (no iOS counterpart); the other eight commits move texts into `ost_visualizer/shared/texts.py` and `ost_visualizer/presentation/texts.py`, unify wording, route error dialogs through one logging helper and add text guards (`tools/check_ui_texts.py`, `tools/check_text_wording.py`). Windows paths below are `d26893c` line numbers, relative to the Windows repository root. Every number was read at `d26893c` by nine analysts (A1 to A9, files in the job scratchpad; the rows of the 18 possible Windows defects, of the owner notes and of the M1 register are A8 and A9).

Outcome of the re-check: of the 65 rows of the earlier lists (the 18 possible Windows defects, the job 5/6 notes, the owner notes a to o) Windows fixed 27 (all in `0a24294`), left 33 unchanged and fixed 5 differently from iOS; iOS had to change in 7 rows (class PORT), kept its own behaviour in 6 (KEEP-iOS), refuses to copy 12 Windows defects that still exist (DEFECT-NOT-COPIED), already matched in 36 and has no counterpart in 4. Of the 112 rows of the M1 register and its further items (A9) 27 are fixed on Windows, 4 changed differently and 81 unchanged (compact table in the M1 section). Where A8 and A9 classify the same item differently (NC-5, NC-10, SS-10, DP-5: "fixed" in A9, "changed differently" in A8, because Windows validates in the use case or the service and not in the writer or the file layer) the difference is only the layer of the fix; the iOS decision is the same.

### Still defects on Windows at `d26893c` (for the Windows side; iOS keeps its safe behaviour and does not copy them)

## Closed by the Windows commit 49a2d59 and the decisions of job 6 (2026-11-21)

Job 6, 2026-11-21. The owner's instruction: "Follow the latest Windows fixes. Branch ios-parity-sync; never main. No force-push. Windows repo read-only. Commits and pushes to the branch are allowed. Failing test first for each code change; beyond those, build and host-test fixes only." Step 0 re-baselined to the Windows repository, which had moved from `b9902e6` through `bf6b8fc` to `49a2d59` (next paragraph); step 1 verified three Windows fixes against the new Windows code first and changed iOS only where it differed (it differed in all three); every Core change was test first (the red runs are below and in `PORTING_LOG.md`, entry "2026-11-21 - Job 6"); step 2 is this file and the other status documents. The owner's text called the commit with the three items "a new commit"; while the job ran it was only an uncommitted working tree on `bf6b8fc`, the plans and the iOS code comments were written against that tree and were re-verified against the commit `49a2d59` ("1.3.3 - 2026-11-21", working tree clean) before the documents were written: every document cites `49a2d59`. Nothing was changed in the Windows repository (`git status` clean before and after; a few Windows functions were executed in memory, read only). The iOS changes were committed on `ios-parity-sync` as `8773287`, `8ef1251`, `bb58e0f`, `fc3a18e`, `e151655` and pushed together (`eae0894..e151655`; never `main`, no force-push). **GitHub run 44953904172 (success, head `e151655`) built and tested the code of job 6**: it compiles and links against the real iPhoneOS SDK and the host tests pass 12 of 12 areas (B-010); the last CI-verified code state is `e151655`. The new Objective-C(++) wiring was syntax-checked against hand-written stubs first (27 of 27 files, 0 errors, 4 stub warnings) and has never run on a device (device checks DEV-09 to DEV-11). Windows paths below are `49a2d59` line numbers, relative to the Windows repository root.

**COND-M08, decided (replaces the "show Linear" decision of 2026-11-16 / 2026-11-19).** Old decision (history; job 5 owner note e): a stored condition type outside 0-3 (for example 7, -1 or 4) showed Linear in the editor (`ost_domain::editor_condition_type`) and a Save rewrote the type like a style change; what Windows did for the thickness was "unverified on Windows". New decision (owner, job 6, following Windows `49a2d59`): such a condition must not crash; the editor opens with a disabled "Unknown (N)" entry selected in the style choice (added after Linear / Area / Count / Attachment, removed when another condition loads) and the layout is built Area-like (Dimensions Thickness / Rise / Run, Appearance without Shape, the Advanced rows of an Area; the iOS-only "Snap to Linear" row of COND-EXTRA-01 shows on every Area-like layout); saving without choosing a type keeps the stored type: a notes-only edit writes only the notes (also with Trim and Curved stored); choosing a real style saves that type with its defaults through the existing `apply_style_change` (nothing is carried from the unknown style; the Spacing is written although unchanged, the generic type-change rule); with takeoffs the style choice stays disabled and the type is kept; anything that only exists for a Linear layout skips controls that were never built. iOS: Core `ConditionChoices.hpp/.cpp` (`unknown_style_type` `.cpp:90`, `condition_style_choices` `:98`, `condition_style_label` `:110`, `editor_layout` `:119`), `ConditionStyleChange.hpp/.cpp` (`condition_form_for_edit` `.cpp:11`; the save paths read the layout from `editor_layout`); Objective-C: `EditConditionViewController.mm` (`_unknownStyleType` is set in `_normalizeLoadedCondition`, `:133-136`, which both load points call; the row builders, `_textForTag` and `_validateAndCollectTextFields` branch on `editor_layout`), `EditConditionViewController_Internal.h`, `EditConditionHelpers.h/.mm` (`styleLabels()` removed), `EditConditionViewController+Pickers.mm` (the Style sheet adds a disabled `UIAlertAction` "Unknown (N)" after Attachment) and `EditConditionViewController+TableView.mm` (the Style row label comes from Core). Tests: 15 added and 3 removed, suite `CondM08_UnknownStyle` (5 in `Config/Tests/Conditions/test_condition_choices.cpp`, 8 in `test_style_change.cpp`, 2 on SQLite level in `test_condition_update.cpp`: the Type column stays an integer 7 / -1); the owner's cases (types 7 and -1: open, edit only the notes, save, only the notes are written, no crash) are `CondM08_UnknownStyle.ANotesOnlyEditWritesOnlyTheNotes` (imperial and metric bid) and `CondM08_UnknownStyle.ANotesOnlySaveWritesOnlyTheNotesColumn` (through the store); removed: `CondM08_UnsupportedType.TheEditorShowsLinear`, `CondM08_UnsupportedType.ASaveRewritesTheTypeLikeAStyleChange` and `CondM08_UnsupportedType.ASaveRewritesTheTypeAndKeepsTheSpacingInAMetricBid`, which encoded the superseded decision. The old note "the Windows result for the thickness is unverified" is obsolete: Windows now has the six tests and the real Windows dialog was run headless for the types 7, -1, 4, 99, INT_MAX and INT_MIN. Status fixed -> fixed-ui-unverified (new Objective-C behaviour that has never run; the Core part is host-tested). Windows differences and possible Windows defects of this item are under "Possible Windows defects" below (note k is the one the owner may want to decide). **Updated 2026-11-21 (job 8):** verified on device: DEV-09 PASS (owner, 2026-11-21, owner's iPhone, IPA of run 44953904172); status fixed again (was fixed-ui-unverified; `statusBeforeDeviceCheck` in `PARITY_DIVERGENCES.json`).

**COND-ELEV-01, the "5'6" rule.** Code: `ost_domain::elevation_text_needs_closing_inch_mark` decides, `parse_elevation_z` calls it; the platform file only shows the alert that `parse_elevation_z` returns, so no Objective-C line changed (its comment was extended). The LOADER and the Core parser are UNCHANGED: a stored name such as "Footing @T 5'6" still reads 60, top (pinned by `CondElev02_ClosingInchMark.TheLoaderStillReadsAStoredFeetDigitNameAsFeet`; the shared parser by `ElevationTextValue.TheGateKeepsTheLoaderQuirks`). Tests: NEW file `Config/Tests/Conditions/test_condition_elevation_foot_mark.cpp` with 14 tests (suite `CondElev02_ClosingInchMark`; one table of 189 rows computed with the real Windows regex and `elevation_text_inches`), and the two tests that encoded the old behaviour were changed: `CondElev01_Validation.AnAcceptedElevationReloadsToTheValueTheDialogSaved` (the `5'6` row removed, the row count 44 -> 40) and `CondElev01_Elevation.TheTextGivesZAndNanIsRefused` (`10'6` is refused now, `10'6"` gives 126.0); comment-only edits in `test_condition_elevation.cpp` (the dialog accepts 90 of the 261 names, was 93) and `Config/Tests/Parsers/test_elevation_text_value.cpp`. Previous behaviour (history): on `b9902e6` the dialog accepted `5'6` as 60 inches and `10'6` as 120 (owner note a of job 5, "report it to the Windows side?"): RESOLVED, Windows fixed it in `49a2d59`; `5'x` is still accepted as 60 inches, by design. The oddities of the new Windows rule are listed under "Possible Windows defects" (ported as is). **Decided by the owner 2026-11-21 (job 7):** oddity (1), a single stray ASCII `"` anywhere in the elevation text switches the closing-inch-mark rule off: recorded as a possible Windows defect and ported as is (confirmed by the owner). **Superseded / updated 2026-11-27 (job 9):** the decision of job 7 is withdrawn: Windows `0a24294` refuses a stray quote and any non-blank character after a foot mark, treats U+2033 and U+201D as inch marks in the parser, the dialog and the loader, and iOS ported all of it (section "Closed by the Windows commits `0a24294`..`d26893c` and the decisions of job 9 (2026-11-27)"). The 189-row table of `CondElev02_ClosingInchMark` was recomputed against the Windows code (45 rows changed, 35 of them flip the acceptance), the 3559-row `ElevationOracleData` was regenerated (144 rows changed, all with U+2033) and `CondElev03_WindowsElevationRuleTests.*` (7 tests) were added.

**DLG-FINITE-01, the guard on new condition numbers.** Windows: `condition_operations.py` `_allocate_condition_identities` (`:42-66`; guard and message `:56-62`): before anything is written, if the highest condition number of the bid plus the number of new conditions would pass 2147483647 it raises `ConditionNumberOverflowError` with `Cannot add N condition(s): the new condition numbers would reach X, past 2147483647, the highest condition number.` (`condition` for N = 1, `conditions` otherwise; X = highest + N); used by `duplicate_conditions` (`:92`, the call `:109-113`), `duplicate_conditions_to_bid` (`:149`) and `insert_condition` (`:254`), each of which re-raises it before its generic handler (`:141-142`, `:241-242`, `:344-345`); `renumber_conditions` (`:743-808`) keeps its temporary numbers above the highest number when they fit, otherwise uses numbers below both the lowest number and 1 (-2, -1, ...) and refuses, writing nothing, only if they would go below -2147483648 (message "Cannot renumber the conditions: no temporary numbers are free above or below the existing ones.", `:777-784`); the write service puts the text into `user_message` (`project_write_service.py:724-727`, `:1005-1006`, `:3587-3592`) and the handlers show it under "Duplicate Conditions" (`condition_action_handler.py:957-962`), "Paste Conditions" (`:1089-1094`), "Duplicate and Reassign Takeoff" (`:951-956`), "Paste" (`plan_view_action_handler.py:4666-4667`) and the New Condition save error (`condition_action_handler.py:650-653`). Windows tests: `tests/infrastructure/mdb/components/test_condition_operations.py` `:1360`, `:1406`, `:1418`, `:1441`, `:1468`, `:1475`, `:1483`, the real-Access test `:2674` (not run here), `tests/application/services/test_project_write_service.py` class `ConditionNumberOverflowResultTests` (5), `test_condition_action_handler.py` (4), `test_plan_view_action_handler.py` (1). iOS: until M1 the ONLY allocator was `ost_store::duplicate_condition` (since M1 (2026-11-24) `insert_condition` allocates condition numbers too) (`Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/Components/ProjectStore+Conditions.cpp:410`; RefNo = highest + 1). It had no guard and read the number with `column_int`: at 2147483647 the copy wrapped to -2147483648 (red run: the call returned a new uid and stored -2147483648). NOW: Core `ost_domain::condition_numbers_overflow_message`, `refuse_new_condition_numbers(highest, count)` and `condition_duplicate_failed_issue(detail)` (`Core/Domain/ConditionFormRules.hpp:110`, `:118`, `:123`; `.cpp:198`, `:207`, `:217`); the store: `duplicate_condition(db_path, bid_uid, condition_uid, std::string *error = nullptr)` (`ProjectStore.hpp:256`) reads `MAX(RefNo)` of the bid as 64 bit INSIDE the transaction after the ownership check (`ProjectStore+Conditions.cpp:467-493`), refuses before any write (no row, no UID consumed; `error` gets the Windows text, every other failure leaves `error` empty) and binds the new number with `bind_int64`; `Services/ImportService.h` / `.mm` `duplicateCondition:inBid:error:` (`.mm:461`); `Controllers/ViewController/Components/ViewController+Conditions.mm` (`:201`) shows the text in the EXISTING failure alert of Duplicate: the title stays "Duplicate Error" (the iOS DBP-11 wording; Windows titles it "Duplicate Conditions" and shows nothing for a plain Duplicate failure without a message), and the generic "Failed to duplicate the condition." stays for every other failure. **Decided by the owner 2026-11-21 (job 7):** the overflow alert of Duplicate keeps the title "Duplicate Error" (Windows titles it "Duplicate Conditions"; the message text is identical to Windows); no change. The boundaries (the owner's three): highest 2147483646 + 1 copy stores 2147483647; highest 2147483647 + 1 is refused with X = 2147483648; two copies at 2147483646 are refused (X = 2147483648, plural; unreachable through the iOS UI, because Duplicate copies ONE condition per request: pinned in Core, `refuse_new_condition_numbers(2147483646, 2)`, and by sequential store calls; decided by the owner 2026-11-21, job 7); a stored number already above the limit (2147483648, 3000000000) blocks too; every row of every table is unchanged after a refusal (`TempDb::snapshot_all`). Other iOS paths that allocate or shift condition numbers (the owner asked for the list): `update_condition` -> `shift_conflicting_ref_nos` (the user types the number; guarded in job 5) and its in-memory twins `ConditionChanges.hpp` and `ProjectData.hpp` (guarded); `parse_condition_number` (user input, capped at 2147483647); `import_insert_raw_row` (copies the stored RefNo of a .ost / .osp verbatim, allocates nothing); `shift_condition_ref_nos` (`RefNo = RefNo + 1`, no production caller (only `test_store_error_propagation.cpp`), no guard: left unchanged on purpose, it creates no new number). iOS had no New Condition when this was written; since M1 (2026-11-24) it has one: `insert_condition` allocates its one condition number through `allocate_condition_identities` and refuses past 2147483647 like `duplicate_condition`, so condition numbers are allocated by `insert_condition` and `duplicate_condition`. iOS still has no paste or copy to another bid, no Duplicate-and-Reassign, no multi-condition duplicate and no Renumber Conditions, so the temporaries rule of `renumber_conditions` has no iOS counterpart (nothing was invented). Tests: 14 (`CondAlloc01_NewNumbers` 3 and `CondAlloc01_DuplicateFailed` 1 in `test_condition_form.cpp`; `CondAlloc01_DuplicateLimit` 10 in `test_condition_duplicate.cpp`). The statements "`duplicate_condition` still computes `COALESCE(MAX(RefNo), 0) + 1` and reads it with `column_int` ... Windows is unguarded too" (end of the DLG-FINITE-01 record below and owner note g) are superseded. Not repaired: older iOS files may already hold -2147483648 (a wrapped duplicate) or 2147483648 (an update shift); the guard does not repair them (Windows has no repair either), and a stored value above INT_MAX still reads as negative on load (`ProjectStore+Load.cpp`, `column_int`).

Mutation checks (Core only; the Objective-C(++) wiring has no host test and could not be mutated): elevation 26 mutants of `ConditionFormRules.cpp` (M01 to M26), 25 killed (one of them survived the first 13 tests and was killed by a 14th test) and 1 equivalent (the rule fed the unstripped text; checked on 579,195 short texts); allocator 9 mutants killed and 1 equivalent by reasoning (`bind_int64` -> `bind_int`, not run: the bound number never exceeds INT_MAX once the guard passed); COND-M08 14 mutants killed, 2 of them survivors of the first 14 tests that were closed by new tests. The work was then reviewed by five independent read-only reviewers (the elevation rule, the allocator, COND-M08 Core, the COND-M08 Objective-C wiring, and requirements coverage with tree hygiene): 0 behaviour defects; their findings were fixed as tests and comments (two overflow cases of the elevation tests, in `CondElev02_ClosingInchMark.ForeignMarksAndMarksWithoutReadableFeetStayRefused`, that could not detect a lost overflow refusal and now pin the refusal of a feet run beyond the float range (its two guards, `feet_digits_to_inches` and the finite check of `parse_elevation_z`, back each other up, so only removing both is caught by those cases); stale Windows line citations in iOS comments (65 comment snippets in 14 files, no line added or removed); comments that cited scratch files; an over-claiming COND-M08 characterisation test; the SQLite notes-only test that lacked the metric row). Reviewer re-derivations: a line-by-line Python translation of the C++ foot-mark loop (not the C++ itself) against the verbatim Windows regex on 14,900,788 strings of length 0-5 plus 3,000,000 random strings (0 differences) and all 189 table rows against the real Windows functions (0 mismatches; the host test runs the rows through the compiled C++); a Python model of `refuse_new_condition_numbers` (not the C++ itself) against the real Windows function over 15 values of the highest number (11 listed entries, one of them the range 2147483644 to 2147483648) and 4 counts (0 differences); the Windows dialog for the types 7, -1, 4, 99, INT_MAX and INT_MIN run headless. Then three independent testers ran 100 mutants on the final tree (elevation rule 41, condition-number guard 30, COND-M08 29): 91 killed after three test gaps were closed by assertions in existing tests (no test added, totals unchanged), 7 equivalent, 2 not killable; the Objective-C(++) wiring cannot be mutation-tested on the host. The final full runs of the finished tree (MSVC 1760 of 1760 and 1769 of 1769 with Manifold, clang 1760 of 1760 and 1769 of 1769 with Manifold, `check_objc` 27 of 27 files, 0 errors, 4 stub warnings) and the full mutation record are in `PORTING_LOG.md`, entry "2026-11-21 - Job 6".

The 9 equivalent mutants of the 174: `< 0x20` against `<= 0x20` (the space is handled by its own case); the `# Display Mode:` MTL header written raw (this equals Windows; the extra `single_line` of iOS there is unobservable with the fixed labels); the size shortcut of `files_have_same_content`; a reversal inside `from_manifold` (undone by `fix_face_winding`); a NULL MAX treated as overflow in the overflow probe (cannot occur: the probe only runs when a row exists); the own-uid filter and the UPDATE's `AND [BidUID] = ?` of `set_layers_visibility` (redundant guards); the layer-less branch of `is_condition_visible` (a layer stored under an empty uid cannot exist); and `<= 0` against `< 0` in `snap_increments_ost`.

Line numbers of iOS files are those of 2026-11-19.

### The 8 former formal items (all kept, no code change; status wont-fix on 2026-11-19; job 5 moved four of them, see each record)

#### 1. DBP-04 / DBP-05 (aliases SNP-01, LP-19, LOAD-01): a stored `TakeoffIncrements` of exactly 0

- **Decision**: a stored `Bids.TakeoffIncrements` of exactly 0 (also 0.0 and -0.0) keeps meaning snapping off on iOS; a NULL value loads as 1.0 like Windows (LOAD-01 is also an alias of DBP-03, which stays as it is).
- **Windows at `6d4786f` (history; `b9902e6` is in the last bullet)**: `ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/hierarchy_reader.py:126` (`float(bid_row.TakeoffIncrements or 1.0)`, the loader the plan view uses) turned 0 into 1.0; `ost_visualizer/presentation/components/plan_view/view.py:3955-3962` (`set_snap_settings`) treats `<= 0` as snapping off; `ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/settings_reader.py:151` and `ost_visualizer/presentation/dialogs/cover_sheet/dialog.py:2385-2388` keep 0. No Windows test pinned a stored 0 through the loader.
- **iOS**: `Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/Components/ProjectStore+Load.cpp:378` keeps a stored value (0 stays 0 = snapping off, `Bid::snap_increments_ost`, `Core/Domain/Bid.hpp:24`: a value <= 0 means off), `:380` loads NULL as 1.0.
- **Pinned by** (updated 2026-11-20, job 5): `Dbp05_TakeoffIncrementsZero.AStoredZeroTurnsSnappingOff` (`Config/Tests/Persistence/test_load_fields.cpp:68`; measure base 0 and 1 against a stored 0, 0.0 and -0.0; called `ZeroKeepsSnappingDisabledUntilTheOwnerDecides` until job 5), `Dbp04_TakeoffIncrements.NullLoadsAsOneInch` (`Config/Tests/Persistence/test_load_fields.cpp:50`), `Dbp04_TakeoffIncrements.SnapIncrementFollowsTheMeasurementSystem` (`Config/Tests/Persistence/test_load_fields.cpp:91`; among its rows (measure base, stored) 0/NULL gives 1, 1/NULL gives 1/25.4, 0/0.25 gives 0.25 and 1/10.0 gives 10/25.4).
- **To follow Windows instead** (updated 2026-11-20, job 5): nothing to do since `b9902e6`: Windows keeps a stored 0 too (last bullet). The 2026-11-19 recipe (load 1.0 for a stored 0) is obsolete.
- **Windows outcome** (updated 2026-11-20, job 5): the 2026-11-19 note (two Windows readers disagreeing on a stored 0) was fixed on Windows in `b9902e6`; no defect is left. Table "Possible Windows defects" below.
- **Superseded / updated 2026-11-20 (job 5)**: Windows `b9902e6` keeps a stored 0: `ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/hierarchy_reader.py:126-130` gives 1.0 only when the column is NULL (`1.0 if bid_row.TakeoffIncrements is None else float(bid_row.TakeoffIncrements)`), and the plan view treats `<= 0` as snapping off (`view.py:3955-3962`, unchanged). Windows tests: `tests/infrastructure/mdb/components/test_hierarchy_reader.py:831-836` (a stored 0 loads as 0.0) and `tests/integration/mdb/test_snap_increment_reaches_plan_view.py:72-93` (a stored 0 turns snapping off for both measurement systems; a missing value keeps the default). So both sides agree and the iOS code did not change (only a comment in `ProjectStore+Load.cpp`): status wont-fix -> no-longer-divergent. The pin test was widened and renamed (see Pinned by). The old phrase "Windows disagrees with itself" is obsolete.

#### 2. BRW-09 (alias DBP-22): restore from Deleted Bids when `OrigBidProjectUID` names a folder that no longer exists

- **Decision**: the bid is restored unassigned (Windows refuses the whole restore group).
- **Windows**: `ost_visualizer/presentation/handlers/project_write_handler.py:580-610` (`restore_bids` groups the bids by original project; the failure dialog "Restore Error", "Failed to restore bids." is at :604-609) and `ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/project_operations.py:38-45` (`move_bids_to_project` refuses a missing target or original project before any change, so the WHOLE restore group fails and the bid stays in the trash). Windows tests pin the refusal: `tests/infrastructure/mdb/components/test_project_operations.py:124` and `:140`.
- **iOS**: `ost_store::restore_bid` (`Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/ProjectStore.cpp:322`) restores such a bid unassigned, which is what the iOS Restore action always did (menu entry `Controllers/DatabaseViewController.mm:571`).
- **Pinned by**: `Brw09_Restore.AfterTheOriginalFolderWasDeletedTheBidComesBackUnassigned` (`Config/Tests/Browser/test_trash_restore.cpp:180`), `Brw09_Restore.InterimADanglingOriginalFolderRestoresTheBidUnassigned` (`Config/Tests/Browser/test_trash_restore.cpp:217`).
- **To follow Windows instead (not planned)**: in `restore_bid`, roll back and return false when `OrigBidProjectUID` is set but the folder row is missing (today the JOIN simply finds no target); `DatabaseViewController.mm:583` already shows the failure alert when the call returns NO. Flip the tests.
- **Possible Windows defect**: none. Judged a deliberate referential-integrity rule: a dangling reference cannot arise inside Windows because deleting a project clears it (`ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/project_operations.py:190-199`). Weakness only for foreign data: such a bid can never be restored on Windows and the dialog blames a locked database.

#### 3. EXP-12: control characters in names of exported OBJ groups, materials and DXF layers

- **Decision** (2026-11-19; replaced in job 5, see the last bullet): control characters (U+0000-U+001F, U+007F) are dropped from those names; Windows kept them.
- **Windows at `6d4786f` (history; `b9902e6` is in the last bullet)**: `ost_visualizer/presentation/visualization/exporters/base_exporter.py:175-201` (`_sanitize_material_name` replaces about 20 punctuation characters and keeps control characters); the name reaches `obj_exporter.py:45` and `:71-72` and `dxf_exporter.py:21-22` (same directory).
- **iOS** (updated 2026-11-20, job 5): `sanitize_material_name` (`Core/Export/export_text.cpp:165`) turns every control character (U+0000-U+001F and U+007F, the `default:` branch at :196-206) into one `_` in the same pass as the other replacements, and the digit-prefix and empty-name rules see the final text (`"\n1x"` gives `_1x`, `"\n"` gives `_`); it is used for the OBJ group and material (`Core/Export/obj_writer.cpp:124`) and for the export material name that is also the DXF layer name (`Core/Meshing/ExportMeshSet.cpp:82`). Everything else equals Windows. Until job 5 iOS dropped the control characters.
- **Pinned by** (updated 2026-11-20, job 5): `ExportText.Exp12_ControlCharactersBecomeUnderscoresLikeWindows` (`Config/Tests/Export/test_export_text.cpp:37`), `ExportText.Exp12_InjectedLinesCannotSurviveInAMaterialName` (`:60`), `ExportText.Exp12_PrintableAndNonAsciiTextIsUnchangedByTheControlCharacterRule` (`:74`), `ExportText.Exp12_TheDigitRuleAndTheEmptyRuleSeeTheReplacedText` (`:87`), `ObjWriter.Exp12_ControlCharactersInANameBecomeUnderscoresInGUsemtlAndNewmtl` (`Config/Tests/Export/test_obj_writer.cpp:282`), `ObjWriter.Exp12_EveryControlCharacterOfANameIsOneUnderscoreInTheFile` (`:314`), `ObjWriter.GroupAndMaterialNamesAreSanitisedLikeWindows` (`:191`), `ExportGroups.Exp12_ControlCharactersOfAConditionNameBecomeUnderscoresInTheMaterialName` (`Config/Tests/Export/test_export_mesh_set.cpp:1018`), `ExportGroups.Exp12_ALineBreakOrNulInAConditionNameInjectsNoLineIntoTheObjMtlOrDxf` (`:1035`; it covers the DXF layer path, which no Windows test covers).
- **To follow Windows instead** (updated 2026-11-20, job 5): done in job 5 (last bullet); the 2026-11-19 recommendation to keep dropping the characters is withdrawn.
- **Windows outcome** (updated 2026-11-20, job 5): fixed on Windows in `b9902e6` (`base_exporter.py:15`, `:200`): the control characters become `_`, so a condition name with a line break no longer injects OBJ, MTL or DXF statements. Table "Possible Windows defects" below.
- **Superseded / updated 2026-11-20 (job 5)**: Windows `b9902e6` writes `_` for each of U+0000-U+001F and U+007F in these names (`ost_visualizer/presentation/visualization/exporters/base_exporter.py:15`, `_CONTROL_CHARACTERS`, applied by `.translate` at :200 after the replace chain; only these characters: U+0085, U+2028, U+2029 and U+00A0 are untouched; the `Mat_` digit prefix and the `Unknown` fallback see the final text; Windows tests `tests/presentation/visualization/exporters/test_base_exporter.py:123-138`). The owner's instruction of job 5 was to follow the fixed Windows behaviour, so the decision of 2026-11-19 (drop them) is replaced: iOS now writes one `_` per character (`"\n1x"` gives `_1x` and `"\n"` gives `_`; iOS gave `Mat_1x` and `Unknown`). The oracle table `Config/Tests/Export/ExportTextOracleData.hpp` has no control character (0 of 569 rows) and was re-verified unchanged against `b9902e6`. The old test `ExportText.SanitizeMaterialNameDropsControlCharactersUnlikeWindows` was removed (its cases live on in `ExportText.Exp12_TheDigitRuleAndTheEmptyRuleSeeTheReplacedText`). Status wont-fix -> fixed.

#### 4. EXP-13 (alias MSH-M02): OBJ face winding when the export holds a negative

- **Decision**: every exported OBJ mesh stays outward-facing, with or without a negative of another type in the export. Same line as the decided EXP-M02, GEO-15 and EXP-17.
- **Windows at `6d4786f` (history; `b9902e6` is in the last bullet)**: `ost_visualizer/presentation/visualization/core/boolean_operations.py:10-12` (`_flip_manifold_faces`) and `:49-61` (without a negative the input is returned as built; otherwise EVERY returned mesh is flipped), `cpp_extensions/src/module_geometry.cpp:95-103` (a positive without a same-type negative comes back untouched, with the generator's winding), `ost_visualizer/presentation/visualization/exporters/adapters/obj_writer.py:38-43` (`_transform_faces` swaps every face). Re-executed for the research: without a negative the wall is +48000 and the slab +40000 (outward); with a negative of another type the untouched wall is -48000 (inward) next to the cut slab +36400 (outward): one Windows OBJ mixes both windings. The only Windows test of the flip mocks `boolean_union` (`tests/presentation/visualization/core/test_boolean_operations.py:11`).
- **iOS** (updated 2026-11-20, job 5): every mesh stays outward-facing (`Core/Meshing/BooleanPass.hpp:59-66` states the rule: the pass never touches the winding; the generators `Core/Meshing/gen_count.cpp:97` and `Core/Meshing/gen_curved.cpp:14`). Nothing in iOS consumes or inverts the old Windows flip (checked in job 5: no face culling, two-sided lighting in `Rendering/Shaders/src/fs_mesh.sc:13` and `fs_oit_accum.sc:13`, the writers write faces as stored).
- **Pinned by** (updated 2026-11-20, job 5): `BooleanRule.Winding_ThePassNeverReversesTheFacesOfAPositive` (`Config/Tests/Export/test_boolean_rule.cpp:164`), `ExportRule.Winding_EveryMeshThatLeavesTheViewAndBothExportsIsOutwardFacing` (`Config/Tests/Export/test_export_mesh_set.cpp:642`; Manifold twin `ExportRuleManifold.Winding_EveryMeshThatLeavesTheViewAndBothExportsIsOutwardFacingWithManifold`, `:650`), `ObjWinding.ExportedMeshesHaveThePositiveSignedVolumeOfTheWindowsObj` (`Config/Tests/Export/test_obj_writer.cpp:503`), `ObjWinding.EveryCurvedWallAndCylinderVariantIsOutwardFacing` (`:568`), `ObjWinding.BooleanDifferenceResultsAreOutwardFacing` (`:599`, Manifold builds only; renamed in job 5 from `BooleanResultsStayOutwardFacingWithANegativeInTheExport`, which only tested `boolean_difference_batch`, not an export).
- **To follow Windows instead (not planned)** (updated 2026-11-20, job 5): not a one-line change, and the 2026-11-19 recipe (reverse the faces of every surviving positive when the scene holds a negative) describes the `6d4786f` rule and is obsolete. Following `b9902e6` literally would need the bounding-box pre-check in `Core/Meshing/BooleanPass.cpp` (an uncut positive comes back as the original mesh with its generator winding, only the result of a positive that may be cut is reversed) and generators and an OBJ writer with the Windows windings; it would contradict the GEO-15, EXP-M02 and EXP-17 decisions. Only needed if byte-identical OBJ vertex and face lists with negatives are wanted.
- **Windows outcome** (updated 2026-11-20, job 5): changed on Windows in `b9902e6`, differently from what the 2026-11-19 suggestion said (a bounding-box rule, not the `boolean_applied` flag): partly fixed on Windows, decided on iOS. A cut positive is still reversed and the generator winding split of EXP-17 remains, so one Windows OBJ can still mix windings: still a possible Windows defect. Table "Possible Windows defects" below.
- **Superseded / updated 2026-11-20 (job 5)**: the premise changed, the decision stands. Windows `b9902e6` no longer reverses every positive when the export holds a negative (the "Windows at `6d4786f`" bullet above is the old rule). `ost_visualizer/presentation/visualization/core/boolean_operations.py:37-111` (helpers :37-67: `_bounds` :37-41, `_bounds_overlap` :44-48, `_positives_with_cut_flags` :51-67; `apply_boolean_operations` :70-111): a positive whose bounding box does not strictly overlap any same-type negative bounding box (`_bounds_overlap`, strict `<` on all three axes, touching counts as apart) is returned as the ORIGINAL mesh with its generator winding (:96-98), and only the native result of a positive that may be cut is reversed (`_flip_manifold_faces` at :99; an empty positive counts as may-be-cut, empty negatives are ignored); `boolean_union` still always reverses (:121). The winding split of the generators (EXP-17) remains and Windows now pins it in its own integration test (`tests/integration/geometry/test_uncut_winding_with_negatives.py:226-245`: in the OBJ a rhombus has volume -1150 and a wall +800). iOS keeps every mesh outward-facing (decided: GEO-15, EXP-13, EXP-M02, EXP-17, MSH-21): no code change; the bounding-box pre-check is not ported (only the vertex and face lists of an uncut same-type positive, which iOS rebuilds through the boolean difference, and the speed differ; inferred, not compared on a real Manifold run of that case). The statements "Windows flips EVERY positive when the export holds a negative" and "one Windows OBJ mixes both windings" because of that flip are obsolete. Status stays wont-fix.
- **Updated 2026-11-27 (parity pass 3)**: the premise changed again and the divergence is gone. Windows `d1c430a` makes every generator outward (`ost_visualizer/presentation/visualization/utils/mesh.py:62`, `outward_faces`) and removes the flip from the boolean pass and the OBJ writer (`exporters/adapters/obj_writer.py:20-25`), so Windows and iOS now agree on the winding of every mesh, with and without a negative; the statements above that one Windows OBJ can mix windings are history. iOS needed no code change; the winding of every shape is pinned (`OutwardWindingPerShape.*`, `OutwardWindingPerShapeManifold.*`, section "Closed by the Windows commit `d1c430a`"). The status of EXP-13 (alias MSH-M02) moves from wont-fix to no-longer-divergent in pass 3 (with EXP-M02 and GEO-15: the counts are now 176 fixed, 23 fixed-ui-unverified, 0 blocked-needs-decision, 13 wont-fix, 1 cannot-reproduce, 7 no-longer-divergent); the decision "every mesh stays outward-facing" stands and is now simply what Windows does too; the one thing the old decision kept apart, the vertex and face ORDER of an exported mesh (the bounding-box shortcut, EXP-17), stays wont-fix.

#### 5. EXP-16: the raw condition name in the OBJ and MTL comment lines

- **Decision** (2026-11-19): CR and LF of a condition name are written as a space in the `# <name>` comment lines of the OBJ and the MTL; Windows wrote the name raw (at `6d4786f`; `b9902e6` does the same as iOS, last bullet).
- **Windows at `6d4786f` (history; `b9902e6` is in the last bullet)**: `ost_visualizer/presentation/visualization/exporters/obj_exporter.py:46` (MTL `# {original_name}`) and `:70` (OBJ), with `original_name` the raw condition name (`base_exporter.py:135-146`). A name with CR or LF ends the comment and the rest becomes OBJ or MTL statements. The Windows tests use only a plain name (`tests/presentation/visualization/exporters/test_obj_exporter.py:23-83`).
- **iOS** (updated 2026-11-20, job 5): `OBJWriter::single_line` (`Core/Export/obj_writer.cpp:131`) writes CR and LF as a space and every other character as it is; used for the OBJ comment line (:168) and the MTL comment line (:206) (and for the "# Display Mode:" header, :199; Windows writes that one raw, unobservable with the fixed mode labels). The rest of the OBJ and MTL text equals Windows byte for byte.
- **Pinned by** (updated 2026-11-20, job 5): `ObjWriter.Exp16_LineBreaksInAConditionNameAreWrittenAsSpacesLikeWindows` (`Config/Tests/Export/test_obj_writer.cpp:336`), `ObjWriter.Exp16_EachCrAndLfOfACommentIsOneSpaceAndNothingElseChanges` (`:374`). The test gap recorded on 2026-11-19 is closed.
- **To follow Windows instead** (updated 2026-11-20, job 5): nothing to do: since `b9902e6` Windows replaces CR and LF in these lines too.
- **Windows outcome** (updated 2026-11-20, job 5): fixed on Windows in `b9902e6` (`obj_exporter.py:9-10`, `:50`, `:74`); a name with a line break no longer defines or selects extra materials. Table "Possible Windows defects" below.
- **Superseded / updated 2026-11-20 (job 5)**: Windows `b9902e6` replaces CR and LF by one space each in the comment lines (`ost_visualizer/presentation/visualization/exporters/obj_exporter.py:9-10`, `_comment_text`, used at :50 for the MTL and at :74 for the OBJ; only CR and LF, not other control characters, as the 2026-11-19 suggestion had it), so iOS matches without a code change (only a comment in `obj_writer.cpp`); Windows test `tests/presentation/visualization/exporters/test_obj_exporter.py:110-150` (`OBJExporterCommentTests`). Status wont-fix -> no-longer-divergent.

#### 6. IMP-20: three sub-behaviours of the DATETIME and binary column rule

- **Decision (all three kept)**: (1) a DATETIME integer outside the C int range (for example `99999999999 1 1 0 0 0`) stores NULL and the import goes on; (2) `BidTexts.Name` and `BidCallOuts.Name` are stored as UTF-8 text; (3) memo and IMAGE (long binary) text columns stay TEXT. The decided date rule itself (a six-part string is kept as text, NULL when it is not a valid date) is done and tested. IMP-05 (an integer of more than 64 bits fails the import, like Windows) is unchanged and was not part of this decision.
- **Windows at `6d4786f` (history; `b9902e6` is in the last bullet)**, all in `ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/`: `import_operations.py:594-610` (catches only `(ValueError, TypeError)` at :608: an `OverflowError` escapes and the whole import fails and rolls back; the Windows test `tests/infrastructure/mdb/components/test_import_operations.py:3079-3084` calls it a characterisation with an open owner question); names as latin-1 bytes: `import_operations.py:614-615` with `serialization.py:5-8` and `:70-81` (`encode_annotation_text`, raises for any character outside latin-1, for example the euro sign); memo and IMAGE text as a UTF-8 BLOB: `import_operations.py:611-616` with `serialization.py:54-67`.
- **iOS**: `Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/Components/ProjectStore+ImportWriter.cpp:153` (`is_valid_datetime`), `:188-196` (date column rule: raw six-part text, else NULL) and `:198-207` (names stay text, memo and IMAGE text kept as TEXT); `Core/Persistence/OstSchema.cpp:1302` (datetime conversion) and `:519` (BidTexts table); `Core/Persistence/OstSchema.hpp:45` (column type contract). Why text: the database layer reads text as UTF-8 only (`Core/Persistence/OstDatabase.cpp:162`, `Statement::column_text`) and iOS never reads those two columns; the writers bind text and the statement layer has no blob binding (`ProjectStore+Takeoffs.cpp:564-567`, updated in job 5).
- **Pinned by** (updated 2026-11-20, job 5): (1) `ImportColumnTypes.Imp20_AnIntegerBeyondTheCIntRangeReadsAsNull` (`Config/Tests/Import/test_import_column_types.cpp:119`), `ImportColumnTypes.Imp20_AnOutOfRangeDateStoresNullAndTheImportGoesOn` (`:349`), `ImportColumnTypes.Imp20_AnOutOfRangeDateAndTheTextNullBothStoreNullAndTheRowIsWritten` (`:373`), `ImportColumnTypes.Imp20_AnythingElseReadsAsNull` (`:99`), `ImportColumnTypes.Imp20_AValidSixPartDateKeepsItsRawText` (`:87`), `ImportColumnTypes.Imp20_TheConversionMatchesTheWindowsFunctionOnEveryOracleValue` (`:158`; the oracle fixture `Config/Tests/Fixtures/import_datetime_oracle.txt` has 3448 rows and no row marked X any more); (2) and (3) `ImportColumnTypes.Imp20_TheColumnTablesAreTheWindowsSchemaColumns` (`:225`), `ImportColumnTypes.Imp20_MemoTextAndDatesOfTheBidTablesAreConverted` (`:327`), `ImportColumnTypes.Imp20_EmptyMemoAndBinaryValuesAreNullAndTextIsKept` (`:200`), `ImportColumnTypes.Imp20_TheTableInfoReportsTheWindowsTypeOfEveryDatetimeAndImageColumn` (`:270`). Test gap for (2): the name columns are only covered through the column tables; no test imports a `BidTexts` or `BidCallOuts` name with a character outside latin-1. The two tests of (1) were called `Imp20_AnIntegerBeyondTheCIntRangeReadsAsNullInsteadOfFailingTheImport` and `Imp20_AnOverflowingDateReadsAsNullAndTheImportGoesOn` until job 5.
- **To follow Windows instead (not planned)**: only needed if an OST or Windows-format export is added or the Windows byte format must be kept. (1) needs nothing any more (Windows `b9902e6` stores NULL too, last bullet); (2) and (3) change the memo / long binary branch of `import_convert_value` (`ProjectStore+ImportWriter.cpp:198-207`) and add blob binding to `OstDatabase`.
- **Windows outcome** (updated 2026-11-20, job 5): (1) fixed on Windows in `b9902e6` (`import_operations.py:608` catches `OverflowError` too); (2b) not fixed: the annotation save path only logs one warning now and still refuses to save latin-1-unsafe text (`annotation_operations.py:178-186`, in `save_annotation_text_properties`; the encoder is called at `:266`, `:476` and `:501`): a possible Windows defect, log message only in `b9902e6`. Judged not a defect: (2) and (3) on the import side (a deliberate legacy storage format). Table "Possible Windows defects" below.
- **Superseded / updated 2026-11-20 (job 5)**: for sub-rule (1) only: Windows `b9902e6` catches `OverflowError` as well (`ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/import_operations.py:608`: `except (ValueError, TypeError, OverflowError)`; Windows tests `tests/infrastructure/mdb/components/test_import_operations.py:796` and `:3099`, `CHANGELOG.md:37-39`), so the sub-rule is parity. The fixture `Config/Tests/Fixtures/import_datetime_oracle.txt` had 176 rows marked X ("an exception escapes"): the current Windows function was run read only on all 3448 rows and returns None for all 176, so X was replaced by N byte for byte and the header updated. Sub-rules (2) and (3) and the raw text of a valid date stay as decided. The item stays wont-fix.
- **Superseded / updated 2026-11-21 (job 6)**: for sub-rule (1): Windows `bf6b8fc` moved the six-part rule into the shared module `ost_visualizer/domain/services/ost_text_values.py` (`parse_ost_datetime` `:15-22`, the `except (AttributeError, ValueError, TypeError, OverflowError)` at `:20`) and the importer calls it at `ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/import_operations.py:597`; the `b9902e6` citation `import_operations.py:608` no longer exists, and the rule did not change (re-read at `49a2d59`). It gives the same NULL / date answer as the stored fixture on all 3448 rows and the iOS `is_valid_datetime` (`ProjectStore+ImportWriter.cpp:153`) agrees on all 3448 rows, on the inputs of the new Windows tests and on 152 further edge inputs (0 differences; the C++ itself runs the 3448 rows in the host test, the other inputs were compared through a Python transcription of the C++ that was read, not run): already matching, no iOS change except the comment citations; 105 of those inputs could be appended to the fixture as data rows (offered, not added: owner note n). The item stays wont-fix for (2) and (3).

#### 7. LP-24: Show All / Hide All on locked template layers

The merge, the Sequence sort and the stable ties of the layer list are done. Only this sub-point was open.

- **Decision**: Show All / Hide All change only the bid's own layers; the locked template layers shared with other bids are never written and a template-only entry cannot be toggled.
- **Windows at `6d4786f` (history; `b9902e6` is in the last bullet)**: `ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/layer_operations.py:259-300` (`update_all_layers_show`, template filter at :270-274) also writes the `Show` of locked TEMPLATE layer rows (shared by every bid), while `update_layer_show` (`:43-55`, guard at :49) refuses a layer of no bid (`ost_visualizer/infrastructure/database/bid_owned_identity.py:205-235`). Windows tests pin both behaviours (`tests/infrastructure/mdb/components/test_layer_operations.py:335`, `:365`, `:630`).
- **iOS** (updated 2026-11-20, job 5): `ost_domain::plan_bulk_layer_visibility` (`Core/Domain/Layer.hpp:91`, `Core/Domain/LayerMerge.cpp:107`) returns the uids of the bid's own layers that differ, with the outcomes NoOwnLayers (fail, write nothing), AlreadyThere and Write; `is_shared_template_layer` (`Layer.hpp:65`, `LayerMerge.cpp:100`) names a shared row; `Controllers/ViewController/Components/ViewController+Layers.mm:95` (`_setAllLayersVisible:`; :108 since parity pass 2) uses the plan; `ost_store::set_layers_visibility` (`Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/Components/ProjectStore+Takeoffs.cpp:84`) keeps the all-uids eligibility check and then writes only rows with BidUID = the bid; memory (`ProjectData::apply_layer_visibility`, `Core/Data/ProjectData.hpp:107`) and the sheet (`is_listed_layer_visible`, :120) follow the plan. A template layer of no bid cannot be toggled with its own checkbox (the store refuses it, a "Layer Visibility" warning is shown, nothing changes in memory); another bid's locked template row is written by the single toggle but memory and sheet stay stale until a reload (owner note b under "Still open"). Until job 5 `layers_to_change_for_bulk_visibility` did the selection and `set_layers_visibility` itself still wrote shared template rows when a caller passed them. **Updated 2026-11-27 (parity pass 3):** the statement that another bid's locked template row "is written by the single toggle but memory and sheet stay stale until a reload (owner note b)" is superseded: the single toggle of such a row is refused by Core (`ost_domain::single_layer_visibility_refusal`) and by the store (`ost_store::set_layer_visibility` writes nothing), with the Windows alert "Layer Visibility" / "This layer is a locked template layer that other bids share, so it cannot be shown or hidden from this bid."; owner note b is closed (section "Closed by the Windows commit `d1c430a`").
- **Pinned by** (updated 2026-11-20, job 5): `Dbp35_BulkVisibilitySelection.CommentsTemplateOnlyAndMergedAwayLayersAreLeftAlone` (`Config/Tests/Persistence/test_layer_bulk.cpp:203`), `Dbp35_BulkVisibilitySelection.OnlyTheLayersThatDifferAreChanged` (`:172`), `Dbp35_SetLayersVisibility.SharedTemplateRowsAreSkipped` (`:316`; called `LockedTemplateLayersAreAccepted` until job 5), `Dbp35_SetLayersVisibility.OnlySharedUidsSucceedAndWriteNothing` (`:328`), `Dbp35_SetLayersVisibility.OwnLockedCopiesAreWrittenSharedRowsAreNot` (`:350`), `Dbp35_SetLayersVisibility.AnotherBidsLockedTemplateRowIsSkippedNotRefused` (`:369`), `Dbp35_SetLayersVisibility.IneligibleUidStillRefusesTheWholeBatchEvenWithSharedUids` (`:382`), `Dbp35_SetLayersVisibility.ALockedRowThatIsNoTemplateRowIsIneligibleAndRefusesTheBatch` (`:400`), `Lp24_SharedTemplateLayer.*` (2, `Config/Tests/Persistence/test_layer_visibility.cpp:206`, `:219`), `Lp24_BulkPlan.*` (3, `:236`, `:272`, `:294`), `Lp24_HideAllAgreement.*` (6, `:430` to `:536`; a port of the Windows `tests/integration/visibility/test_hide_all_shared_layers.py:153`, memory, sheet and reload agree).
- **To follow Windows instead** (updated 2026-11-20, job 5): done in job 5 (last bullet): the Windows rule is the iOS rule now. The 2026-11-19 recipe (let the selection return template rows and let the write accept them) would undo it.
- **Windows outcome** (updated 2026-11-20, job 5): fixed on Windows in `b9902e6`: Hide All and Show All skip shared locked template rows (`layer.py:89-94`, `layer_operations.py:259-371`, `project_data_service.py:665-700`, `ui_event_coordinator.py:6661-6754`), so the shared row is no longer written by Hide All. Table "Possible Windows defects" below.
- **Superseded / updated 2026-11-20 (job 5)**: Windows `b9902e6` leaves shared locked template rows alone on Hide All and Show All: `ost_visualizer/domain/entities/layer.py:89-94` (`is_shared_template_layer`: locked template layer of no bid or of another bid), `ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/layer_operations.py:259-371` (`update_all_layers_show` checks all captured uids, then writes only rows with `[BidUID] = ?`), `ost_visualizer/domain/services/project_data_service.py:665-700` (a condition's `layer_visible` is true unless its layer is one of the bid's own; the pages are untouched when the Image layer is shared), `ost_visualizer/presentation/coordinators/ui_event_coordinator.py:6661-6754` (the previous visibility is taken without shared rows, an empty set returns False; the sidebar path ignores that result); Windows tests `tests/infrastructure/mdb/components/test_layer_operations.py:335` and `tests/integration/visibility/test_hide_all_shared_layers.py:153`. iOS changed in three places (see the iOS bullet) and followed the Windows rule: status wont-fix -> fixed. A bid with only shared layers: Hide All fails, changes nothing and stays silent in the UI, like the Windows sidebar path (owner note c). The decision text above ("never written") now also holds for the store. **Updated 2026-11-27 (job 9):** "stays silent in the UI" is superseded: Windows `0a24294` now shows the alert "Layer Visibility" / "This bid has no layers of its own to show or hide." for Show All and Hide All in a bid with no layer of its own (`ost_visualizer/presentation/coordinators/ui_event_coordinator.py:6810-6820`, `:6846-6853`; `ost_visualizer/presentation/texts.py:1321-1324`), and iOS shows it too (`ViewController+Layers.mm`, commit `371c484`, fixed-ui-unverified; owner note c is answered).

#### 8. RPV-29: the view-dependent normal flip in the 3D lighting

- **Decision**: option (a), keep the flip; the shaders are not changed.
- **Windows**: `ost_visualizer/presentation/visualization/utils/mesh.py:70-80` (`_normalize_vector`: `if nz < 0.0` negate, at :78-79, so horizontal faces are always lit as facing up; used through `prepare_vertices_for_shading`) and `cpp_extensions/src/renderer/renderer.cpp:616-626` and `:652-658` (fragment shaders: raw normal, `max(dot, 0)`, no flip, no culling).
- **iOS**: `Rendering/Shaders/src/fs_mesh.sc:13` and `Rendering/Shaders/src/fs_oit_accum.sc:13` flip the normal towards the viewer (`if (dot(N, V) < 0.0) N = -N;`). The lighting constants and directions are the Windows ones (`Rendering/Renderer.mm:590-592`: ambient 0.55, key 0.45, fill 0.35, rim 0.2).
- **Why the flip stays**: deleting it does not reproduce Windows. The Windows normal is +z-oriented and winding-dependent on vertical faces, while Windows meshes are inward-facing and iOS meshes outward-facing by the GEO-15, EXP-M02 and MSH-21 decisions (for the far bottom face of a transparent box: 1.012 with the flip, 0.691 without, per the fixer's calculation).
- **Pinned by**: nothing: not host-testable (no `shaderc`, no Metal device here); the look was never compared on a device.
- **To follow Windows instead (not planned)**: (b) literal Windows shader: delete the flip in both shaders; (c) replace it by `if (N.y < 0.0) N = -N` (the Y-up form of `nz < 0`). Both need the two generated shader headers (`Rendering/Shaders/generated/fs_mesh.h`, `fs_oit_accum.h`) regenerated with the `shaderc` of the pinned bgfx commit (`BUILD.md`) and a look on a device.
- **Possible Windows defect**: none of its own. Judged deliberate and test-pinned (`mesh.py:70-80`; `tests/presentation/visualization/utils/test_mesh.py:162-168`); the winding-dependent lighting on Windows follows from the winding inconsistency recorded under EXP-13, EXP-17 and EXP-M02 in the table below.
- **Updated 2026-11-27 (parity pass 3)**: unchanged, KEEP-iOS, nothing to port. Windows `d1c430a` still lights with vertex normals (`nz >= 0` forced, 60 degree smoothing, no view flip: `mesh.py:70-80`) and its outward windings now fix the side faces; the sentence above that the winding-dependent lighting follows from the winding inconsistency of EXP-13, EXP-17 and EXP-M02 is history (the inconsistency is gone). iOS keeps the viewer-facing normal (HTML writer `Core/Export/threejs_writer.cpp`, the 3D view flips N toward the viewer): for closed outward meshes both light the visible side from the side facing the light; the residual is back faces, undersides and smoothing (MSH-21, EXP-20 kept). A note for M3 only.

### The four code changes (decided 2026-11-19, test first; code changes 1 and 2 replaced and 3 extended in job 5)

The rule for these changes was a failing test first; the failing-first output of all four is recorded below. All four were independently reviewed, each with a mutation check on a copy that was restored byte-identically (COND-STYLE-02: production code correct, one test gap closed by the reviewer with a new test; COND-ELEV-01, DLG-FINITE-01 and UND-09: no defect found). For UND-09 the red run was against the old rule placed in Core, not against the `.mm`. The platform changes are `.mm` code that was syntax-checked against hand-written stubs and has since compiled against the real iPhoneOS 18.5 SDK on GitHub (2026-11-20 UTC); it has not been run (this describes the 2026-11-19 version: the job 5 edits of `EditConditionViewController.mm` and `.h`, `EditConditionViewController+TableView.mm`, `ViewController+Conditions.mm` and `Services/ImportService.h` / `.mm` were syntax-checked against stubs and have since compiled against the real SDK on GitHub (run 44856186199, success, 2026-11-21 UTC); none had run on a device when this was written, see B-010 and owner note i). **Updated 2026-11-21 (job 8):** the wiring of COND-STYLE-02 (DEV-03), COND-ELEV-01 (DEV-04, DEV-10) and DLG-FINITE-01 (DEV-05, DEV-11) was seen running and passed on the owner's iPhone; the wiring of UND-09 has not been checked on a device.

**Superseded / updated 2026-11-20 (job 5):** code changes 1 and 2 were replaced by following Windows `b9902e6`: each has a last bullet "Superseded / updated 2026-11-20 (job 5)" with the current rule, and its other bullets are the state of 2026-11-19 unless they say "updated". Code change 3 gained its condition-number half, code change 4 is unchanged. The failing-first and review notes below describe the work of 2026-11-19; the work of job 5 was reviewed independently by four reviewers (no blocker; the two majors were fixed) and, for the Core changes, mutation-checked (a clean re-check of 174 mutants over three chains in fresh scratch copies: 158 killed, 9 equivalent, 7 survived, six of those 7 closed afterwards by assertions in existing tests; see "Mutation checks of job 5" under "Closed by the Windows commit b9902e6 and the decisions of 2026-11-20" and `PORTING_LOG.md`; the Objective-C(++) wiring has no host test, could not be mutated, was reviewed by reading and syntax-checked against stubs, and was compiled against the real SDK by run 44856186199; it had never run when this was written; **updated 2026-11-21 (job 8):** code changes 1, 2 and 3 (COND-STYLE-02, COND-ELEV-01, DLG-FINITE-01) passed their device checks, see the paragraph "Updated 2026-11-21 (job 8)" at the top of this file).

**Superseded / updated 2026-11-21 (job 6):** code change 2 (COND-ELEV-01) and code change 3 (DLG-FINITE-01) got a last bullet for job 6 (the dialog's closing-inch-mark rule; the guard on new condition numbers), and the note of code change 1 that COND-M08 is unverified on Windows is replaced (COND-M08 is decided, see the section "Closed by the Windows commit `49a2d59` and the decisions of job 6"). The iOS line numbers of tests in these records are the numbers of job 5: seven Conditions test files changed in job 6 (`test_condition_choices.cpp`, `test_condition_duplicate.cpp`, `test_condition_elevation.cpp`, `test_condition_elevation_dialog.cpp`, `test_condition_form.cpp`, `test_condition_update.cpp`, `test_style_change.cpp`), the support header `ConditionsTestSupport.hpp` changed too, three other test files had comments changed (`Formatting/test_style_defaults.cpp`, `Import/test_import_column_types.cpp`, `Parsers/test_elevation_text_value.cpp`, the last with three lines inserted) and `test_condition_elevation_foot_mark.cpp` was added, so find a test by its name (`grep -rn "TEST(Suite, Name)" Config/Tests`), not by its line; the iOS production line numbers that moved in job 6 were renumbered where a record cites them (the bullet says so).

#### Code change 1: COND-STYLE-02, a style change in a metric bid (decided 2026-11-19 as a deviation from Windows; aligned with Windows in job 5)

- **Decision** (2026-11-19, replaced in job 5, see the last bullet): on a style change in a METRIC bid `style_dimension_defaults(type, metric)` is persisted (spacing 100 mm, Area 150 mm; thickness 100 / 100 / 100 / 0 mm for Linear / Area / Count / Attachment), not the imperial table that Windows wrote at `6d4786f` (`ost_visualizer/presentation/dialogs/edit_condition_dialog.py:1445-1448`). A deliberate deviation from the literal Windows behaviour; the imperial behaviour was unchanged and equal to Windows. The spacing rule of decision (a) of 2026-11-16 (persist the type default spacing unless edited) was unchanged then.
- **Files** (updated 2026-11-20, job 5): `Core/Domain/ConditionStyleChange.hpp` and `.cpp` (in the `.cpp`, lines renumbered in job 6: `type_change_defaults(type, metric)` at :149, `condition_to_save(stored, form, metric)` at :172, `condition_changes_for_save(stored, form, metric)` at :268), `Controllers/EditConditionViewController/EditConditionViewController.mm:370-371` (renumbered in job 6; the single call site, with the dialog's `_metric`), `Config/Tests/Conditions/test_style_change.cpp`, `Config/Tests/Conditions/test_condition_form.cpp`.
